denotes the Jacobian of the coordinate transformation from physical to gyrocentre coordinates (restricted to space and velocity coordinates)

\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\det\frac{\partial\tilde{{\bm{z}}}}{\partial{\bm{z}}}=\frac{B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}{m_{s}},

(4.6)

which is derived in
Appendix

F
and can be written explicitly by making use of
Eq.

3.66
.

We find that the gyrocentre EOMs, as given by
Eq.

3.69
, imply that phase-space volume is conserved.
A proof is given in
Appendix

G
.

Theorem 2
(Gyrocenter Liouville theorem)

Phase-space volume is conserved

\frac{\partial\mathfrak{J}_{s}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot(\mathfrak{J}_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}})+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{\shortparallel}}(\mathfrak{J}_{s}\dot{U}_{\shortparallel})=0.

(4.7)

Furthermore, integrals of the form
Eq.

4.4
can be expressed in terms of the initial phase-space coordinates in the following way

\int f_{s}\mathcal{F}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z=\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})\mathcal{F}({\bm{Z}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}))\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0},

(4.8)

where an absence of arguments implies evaluation at
{\bm{z}},t
.

By combining
Eqs.

4.3
and
4.7
we find the conservative form of the Vlasov equation

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(f_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s})+\nabla\bcdot(f_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}})+\frac{\partial}{\partial u_{\shortparallel}}(f_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\dot{U}_{\shortparallel})=0.

(4.9)

Note that integration of the conservative form of the Vlasov equation over velocity space and subsequent summation over the species
s
, results in the free charge continuity equation

\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{f}}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{f}}=0,

(4.10)

where the gyrocentre free charge and current density are defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{f}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,

(4.11a)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{f}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}}\,\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,

(4.11b)

respectively.

4.2
Low’s action

We use a variational formulation in order to obtain a structure preserving self-consistent Vlasov–Maxwell system of equations.
Such a variational formulation is in particular suitable for our foreseen structure preserving discretization using finite element exterior calculus (FEEC)
(
Kraus et al. 2017
)
.

The starting point is Low’s action
(
Low 1958
)
in gyrocentre coordinates

\mathfrak{A}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int\mathfrak{L}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(4.12a)

where the field Lagrangian is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})L_{s}({\bm{Z}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}),\dot{{\bm{Z}}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}))\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}\int\lvert{\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x.

(4.12b)

Here integration over the time coordinate
t
is done over the interval
[t^{0},t^{1}]
, where
t^{1}
denotes the final time,
L_{s}
denotes the gyrocentre Lagrangian corresponding to the species
s
and
\mu_{0}
denotes the magnetic permeability in vacuum.
Note that we neglect the contribution due to the electric field, as we consider the limit of quasi-neutrality
\epsilon_{0}\rightarrow 0
, thereby eliminating the fast plasma waves which would otherwise be present.
This approximation is in accordance with the low frequency approximation
\varepsilon_{\omega}\ll 1
of the first-order generating function (
3.54
).

We may transform the first integral in
Eq.

4.12b
by making use of
Theorem

2
.
Rather than transforming the integral resulting from each of the contributions of the gyrocentre Lagrangian, we split the gyrocentre Lagrangian in two parts
L_{s}=L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}+L_{s}^{\mathrm{field}}
referred to as the particle and field part, respectively.
Subsequently, we transform and linearize the contribution from the field part

\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})L_{s}({\bm{Z}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}),\dot{{\bm{Z}}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}))\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
\approx\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}({\bm{Z}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}),\dot{{\bm{Z}}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}))\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\int f^{0}_{s}L_{s}^{\mathrm{field}}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(4.13)

where we split the particle distribution function into its initial background part and time-dependent part

f_{s}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel},\mu,t)=f^{0}_{s}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel},\mu)+\delta f_{s}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel},\mu,t),

(4.14)

with
\delta f_{s}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel},\mu,t^{0})=0
.
Furthermore, we have defined the (unperturbed) guiding-centre Jacobian as (cf.
Eq.

4.6
)

\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{B_{0}}{m_{s}},

(4.15)

where we have neglected the
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\delta})
and
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B})
terms from the Jacobian.
Note that this a modelling choice which does not break the structure of the resulting equations.
The Lagrangian as given by
Eq.

4.12b
is now approximated by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}({\bm{Z}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}),\dot{{\bm{Z}}}(t;{\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0}))\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}

(4.16)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}L_{s}^{\mathrm{field}}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z-\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}\int\lvert{\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x.

The field part of the Lagrangian does not affect the EOMs of the particles directly, but will only affect the potentials via the field equations, which are derived in
Section

4.3.2
.
The reason for splitting the Lagrangian in this way, is to simplify the resulting discretized model.
For instance, we want to obtain linear field equations, and thus have linearized the corresponding field part of the Lagrangian.
Note that neglecting the time-dependent part
\delta f_{s}
of the particle distribution function is justified only if
\delta f_{s}
is small compared to
f^{0}_{s}
, which is the case e.g. when studying microturbulence in the core of fusion devices
(
Garbet et al. 2010
)
.

We recall that the gyrocentre single-particle phase-space Lagrangian is given by
Eqs.

2.21
,
3.45
and
3.57
; the following splitting is considered

L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}({\bm{Z}},\dot{{\bm{Z}}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=q_{s}{\bm{A}}_{s}^{\star}\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}+\frac{m_{s}M}{q_{s}}\dot{\Theta}-H_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}({\bm{Z}}),\quad L_{s}^{\mathrm{field}}({\bm{z}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-H_{s}^{\mathrm{field}}({\bm{z}})

(4.17a)

for

H_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}({\bm{Z}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{s}}{2}(U_{\shortparallel}^{\star})^{2}+M^{\star}B_{0}+q_{s}\phi_{1}-q_{s}\rho\langle\!|\mathring{E}^{\varsigma}_{1,\rho}|\!\rangle

(4.17b)

and

H_{s}^{\mathrm{field}}({\bm{z}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{\mu}{2B_{0}}\lvert{\bm{B}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}-\frac{m_{s}}{2q_{s}^{2}B_{0}^{2}}\lvert{\bm{F}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2},

(4.17c)

where we have defined the effective gyrocentre parallel velocity

U_{\shortparallel}^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=U_{\shortparallel}-\langle G_{1,U}\rangle=U_{\shortparallel}-(1-\xi_{R})\frac{q_{s}\rho}{m_{s}}\langle\!|\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\tau}|\!\rangle

(4.18)

as well as the effective gyrocentre magnetic moment

M^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=M-\langle G_{1,M}\rangle=M\left(1+\frac{\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle}{B_{0}}\right)

(4.19)

by making use of
Eq.

3.55
.

The effective parallel velocity coincides with the gyrocentre parallel velocity
U_{\shortparallel}
if
\xi_{R}=1
and approximately coincides with the guiding-centre parallel velocity if
\xi_{R}=0
, thereby allowing for a physical interpretation regardless of the value of
\xi_{R}
.
Similarly, the effective magnetic moment approximately coincides with the guiding-centre magnetic moment.
Note that in our coordinates the magnetic moment transforms as

\langle\bar{M}(\bar{\bar{{\bm{Z}}}})\rangle=M^{\star}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\delta}^{2})\quad\implies\quad\bar{\bar{M}}\approx\frac{m_{s}\bar{u}_{\tau}^{2}}{2(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle)}

(4.20)

by making use of
Eqs.

3.55
and
4.19
.

4.3
Principle of least action

In what follows, we compute the EOMs for the gyrocentre characteristic
{\bm{Z}}
as well as the field equations for the potentials
\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1}
.
We follow the principle of least action, which states that the EOMs and field equations are stationary points of the action.
The following notation is introduced for computing the variation of the action w.r.t. the gyrocentre coordinate

\frac{\delta\mathfrak{A}}{\delta{\bm{Z}}}\left[{\bm{\delta}}\right]\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\mathfrak{A}({\bm{Z}}+\varepsilon{\bm{\delta}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1}),

(4.21)

which we define analogously for the other arguments of the action.

4.3.1
Equations of motion

The EOMs are defined by setting the variation of the action w.r.t. the gyrocentre coordinate to zero, for all suitable trajectories
{\bm{\delta}}
with
{\bm{\delta}}(t^{0})={\bm{\delta}}(t^{1})={\bm{0}}_{6}
.
It follows that the trajectories satisfy

\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})\Biggl[\left(\frac{\partial L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}}{\partial{\bm{Z}}}-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\frac{\partial L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}}{\partial\dot{{\bm{Z}}}}\right)\bcdot{\bm{\delta}}+\underbrace{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\frac{\partial L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}}{\partial\dot{{\bm{Z}}}}\bcdot{\bm{\delta}}\right)}_{=0}\Biggr]\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t=0

(4.22)

by making use of partial integration in time.
As this must hold for all trajectories
{\bm{\delta}}
it follows that the EOMs satisfy the Euler–Lagrange equations (see e.g.
Eq.

2.29
), and therefore the EOMs will be of the form given by
Eq.

3.69
, except that only the particle part of the Hamiltonian, as defined in
Eq.

4.17b
, is used on the right-hand side.

We now compute the required partial derivatives of
H_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}
.
The term appearing in the EOMs given by
Eq.

3.69
can be written as

\displaystyle\nabla H_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}+q_{s}\frac{\partial{\bm{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}}{\partial t}={}

\displaystyle M\nabla(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle)-q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}

(4.23)

\displaystyle-(1-\xi_{R})q_{s}\left[U_{\shortparallel}^{\star}\nabla(\rho\langle\!|\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\tau}|\!\rangle)-\langle\!|(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{E}}_{1})\times{\bm{\rho}}|\!\rangle\right]

by substituting
Eqs.

4.17b
,
3.59
,
3.26
and
4.18
,
and by defining the FLR corrected electric field as

{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}={\bm{E}}_{1}+\nabla\langle\!|\rho\mathring{E}^{\varsigma}_{1,\rho}|\!\rangle+\langle\!|(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{E}}_{1})\times{\bm{\rho}}|\!\rangle=\langle\mathring{{\bm{E}}}_{1}\rangle+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B}),

(4.24)

where the second equality results from
Eq.

D.10
.
Furthermore, we made use of Faraday’s equation

\frac{\partial{\bm{B}}_{1}}{\partial t}=-\nabla\times{\bm{E}}_{1},

(4.25)

which follows from the definition of the electromagnetic fields (
3.26
) and also holds for the FLR corrected fields:
\partial{\bm{\mathcal{B}}}_{1}/\partial t=-\nabla\times{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}
.

For
\xi_{R}=1
we therefore have a full understanding of the terms on the right-hand side of
Eq.

4.23
.
This choice yields a physically meaningful
{\bm{\mathcal{B}}}_{1}
as well as
U_{\shortparallel}^{\star}=U_{\shortparallel}
.
For those reasons we now choose
\xi_{R}=1
.
We recall that we have chosen
\xi_{\Theta}=0
to ensure that
M
is an invariant.
Both choices combined lead to the following symplectic part of the gyrocentre Lagrangian

(\xi_{R},\xi_{\Theta})=(1,0)\quad\implies\quad\bar{\bar{{\bm{\gamma}}}}_{1,{\bm{R}}}=\langle\bar{{\bm{\gamma}}}_{1,{\bm{R}}}\rangle=q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}=q_{s}\left({\bm{A}}_{1}+\langle\!|\mathring{{\bm{B}}}^{\varsigma}_{1}\times{\bm{\rho}}|\!\rangle\right),

(4.26)

where we have substituted
Eq.

3.59
, and note that all other components of
\bar{\bar{{\bm{\gamma}}}}_{1}
are equal to zero.

The second partial derivative that is required for the EOMs is the one w.r.t. the parallel velocity and is given by

\frac{\partial H_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}}{\partial U_{\shortparallel}}=m_{s}U_{\shortparallel},

(4.27)

where we have made use of
Eq.

4.18
with
\xi_{R}=1
.

Substitution of these results in
Eq.

3.69
yields (we only show the relevant and non-trivial EOMs)

\displaystyle\dot{{\bm{R}}}

\displaystyle=U_{\shortparallel}{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}-\frac{1}{q_{s}B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\left[q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}-M\nabla(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle)\right],

(4.28a)

\displaystyle\dot{U}_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{s}}{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}\bcdot\left[q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}-M\nabla(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle)\right],

(4.28b)

where we recall that
{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}
is defined in
Eq.

3.65
.
When substituting
Eq.

3.65
we find that the EOM for the gyrocentre position
{\bm{R}}
can be written as

\displaystyle\dot{{\bm{R}}}={}

\displaystyle U_{\shortparallel}\biggl({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+\overbrace{\frac{{\bm{\mathcal{B}}}_{1,\perp}}{B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}}^{\text{\parbox{39.83368pt}{\centering magnetic flutter\@add@centering}}}\biggr)+\frac{1}{q_{s}B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}\biggl[\overbrace{q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}}^{\text{ExB drift}}-\overbrace{M\nabla(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle)}^{\text{grad-B drift}}+\overbrace{m_{s}U_{\shortparallel}^{2}{\bm{\kappa}}}^{\text{curvature drift}}

(4.29)

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{m_{s}MU_{\shortparallel}}{q_{s}}(\nabla\times{\bm{w}}_{0})\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}}_{\text{gyro-gauge invariance}}\biggr]\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0},

where we have indicated the physical meaning of each of the terms.
The EOM for the gyrocentre parallel velocity
U_{\shortparallel}
as given by
Eq.

4.28b
contains two contributions: an acceleration due to the perturbed parallel component of the FLR corrected electric field
{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}
as well as the contribution due to the magnetic mirror force.

It should be noted that letting
{\bm{\mathcal{B}}}_{1}=\langle\mathring{{\bm{B}}}_{1}\rangle
and/or
{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}=\langle\mathring{{\bm{E}}}_{1}\rangle
implies that the model no longer results from an action principle, thereby resulting in a loss of energy conservation.
Even if
\varepsilon_{B}=0
one must be aware that the identity given by
Eq.

4.24
results from application of the gradient theorem (
D.10
), which is not likely to hold numerically.

4.3.2
Field equations

We give Low’s action explicitly for our parameter choice
(\xi_{R},\xi_{\Theta})=(1,0)
such that we can find the field equations by computing the appropriate variations

\mathfrak{A}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1})=\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})\biggl[q_{s}\left({\bm{A}}_{0,s}^{\star}+{\bm{A}}_{1}+\langle\!|\mathring{{\bm{B}}}^{\varsigma}_{1}\times{\bm{\rho}}|\!\rangle\right)\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}+\frac{m_{s}M}{q_{s}}\dot{\Theta}-\frac{m_{s}}{2}U_{\shortparallel}^{2}\\
-M(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle)-q_{s}\phi_{1}+q_{s}\rho\langle\!|\mathring{E}^{\varsigma}_{1,\rho}|\!\rangle\biggr]\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}\biggl[\frac{m_{s}}{2B_{0}^{2}}\lvert{\bm{E}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}\\
-\frac{m_{s}u_{\shortparallel}}{B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot({\bm{E}}_{1}\times{\bm{B}}_{1})-\left(\mu B_{0}-m_{s}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}\right)\frac{\lvert{\bm{B}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}}{2B_{0}^{2}}\biggr]\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t-\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}\int\lvert{\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(4.30)

where we have substituted
Eqs.

4.17
,
3.59
and
4.16
into
Eq.

4.12a
.
Recall that the electromagnetic fields are defined in
Eq.

3.26
.

Each of the field equations can now be derived by setting the corresponding variation w.r.t. the function to zero.
We start by computing the quasi-neutrality equation, which results from setting the variation of Low’s action (
4.30
) w.r.t. the scalar potential
\phi_{1}
to zero.
That is, the quasi-neutrality equation is derived from

\frac{\delta\mathfrak{A}}{\delta\phi_{1}}\left[\Lambda\right]=0,

(4.31)

where
\Lambda
is a scalar test function.
We note that the substitution
\phi_{1}\mapsto\phi_{1}+\varepsilon\Lambda
results in

{\bm{E}}_{1}\mapsto{\bm{E}}_{1}-\varepsilon\nabla\Lambda.

(4.32)

This results in the following quasi-neutrality equation

-\sum_{s}\int{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z,

(4.33)

where we have used Liouville’s theorem to transform the integral over
f_{s}
, assumed our test function to be independent of time, and have defined the electric polarization as

{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int f^{0}_{s}{\bm{P}}_{1,s}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,\quad{\bm{P}}_{1,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{s}}{q_{s}B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{F}}_{1,\perp},

(4.34)

where we recall that
{\bm{F}}_{1}
denotes the Lorentz force as defined in
Eq.

3.51
.
In simplifying the right-hand side of
Eq.

4.33
we have made use of the gradient theorem (
D.8
)

\Lambda+\rho\langle\!|{\bm{\hat{\rho}}}\bcdot\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\Lambda|\!\rangle=\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle.

(4.35)

We note that, because the gradient theorem in this form will not hold numerically, it is important to discretize the gyro-average using the left-hand side of
Eq.

D.8
, whenever gauge invariance is to be preserved numerically.

Ampère’s law is derived by imposing

\frac{\delta\mathfrak{A}}{\delta{\bm{A}}_{1}}\left[{\bm{\Lambda}}\right]=0,

(4.36)

where
{\bm{\Lambda}}
is a vector-valued test function, and we note that the substitution
{\bm{A}}_{1}\mapsto{\bm{A}}_{1}+\varepsilon{\bm{\Lambda}}
results in

{\bm{E}}_{1}\mapsto{\bm{E}}_{1}-\varepsilon\frac{\partial{\bm{\Lambda}}}{\partial t},\quad{\bm{B}}_{1}\mapsto{\bm{B}}_{1}+\varepsilon\nabla\times{\bm{\Lambda}}.

(4.37)

This results in

\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\int({\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1})\bcdot(\nabla\times{\bm{\Lambda}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x=\sum_{s}\int\left(\frac{\partial{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}}{\partial t}+\nabla\times{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}\right)\bcdot{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\\
+\sum_{s}\int f_{s}\biggl(q_{s}\left[{\bm{\Lambda}}+\nabla\langle\!|\rho\mathring{\Lambda}^{\varsigma}_{\rho}|\!\rangle+\langle\!|(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}})\times{\bm{\rho}}|\!\rangle\right]\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}-\mu\langle\!\langle(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}})_{\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\biggr)\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z,

(4.38)

where we made use of partial integration in time, substituted
Eqs.

4.15
and
4.9
,
and have defined the magnetization as

{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int f^{0}_{s}{\bm{M}}_{1,s}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,\quad{\bm{M}}_{1,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-u_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{P}}_{1,s}-\frac{\mu}{B_{0}}{\bm{B}}_{1,\perp}.

(4.39)

We define the rest-frame magnetic and electric dipole moments per particle as

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathfrak{m}}}_{s}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-\mu\left({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+\frac{{\bm{B}}_{1,\perp}}{B_{0}}\right),

(4.40a)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathfrak{p}}}_{1,s}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{s}}{q_{s}B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{F}}_{1,\perp}=\frac{m_{s}}{B_{0}^{2}}\left({\bm{E}}_{1,\perp}+u_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{B}}_{1}\right),

(4.40b)

where we have included the intrinsic guiding-centre magnetic moment
-\mu{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}

(
Bittencourt 2004
, Eq. (4.35))
.
It follows that the magnetic and electric dipole moments per particle are given by

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathfrak{M}}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathfrak{m}}}_{s}-{\bm{v}}_{0}\times{\bm{\mathfrak{p}}}_{1,s},

(4.41a)

\displaystyle{\bm{P}}_{1,s}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathfrak{p}}}_{1,s}+\frac{1}{c^{2}}\underbrace{{\bm{v}}\times{\bm{\mathfrak{m}}}_{s}}_{=0}

(4.41b)

by making use of
Eqs.

4.39
and
4.34
.
Here
c=1/\sqrt{\epsilon_{0}\mu_{0}}
denotes the speed of light, and we have defined the velocity
{\bm{v}}
as

{\bm{v}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+\frac{{\bm{B}}_{1,\perp}}{B_{0}}\right)u_{\shortparallel},

(4.42)

which includes the contribution from the magnetic flutter as found in
Eq.

4.29
.
The expressions for the magnetic and electric dipole moments per particle can now directly be compared to the those found in
(
Brizard & Hahm 2007
, Eqs. (34) and (35))
as well as to the expressions of a moving electric and magnetic dipole described in
(
Hnizdo 2012
;
Fisher 1971
)
.

Remark 1

In obtaining
Eq.

4.38
we have made use of partial integration in time, thereby omitting the following term from the variation of the action

\displaystyle\frac{\delta\mathfrak{A}}{\delta{\bm{A}}_{1}}\left[{\bm{\Lambda}}\right]={}

\displaystyle\int(\text{Amp\`{e}re's law})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t-\sum_{s}q_{s}\int\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left(f_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\rho\langle\!|\mathring{\Lambda}^{\varsigma}_{\rho}|\!\rangle\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle-\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}\frac{m_{s}}{q_{s}B_{0}^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}({\bm{F}}_{1,\perp}\bcdot{\bm{\Lambda}})\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

(4.43)

We explicitly state this term as it plays a crucial role in the derivation of the conserved energy in
Section

4.7
.

4.4
Strong formulation of the macroscopic Maxwell’s equations

The previously discussed field equations were given in a weak formulation, which is how they naturally arise from the variational formulation.
The weak formulation is exactly what we need for a future FEEC
(
Kraus et al. 2017
)
discretization of the field equations, however, when it comes to physical interpretation it is not the most convenient way to present the equations.
To this end, we now consider the strong formulation of the field equations, where we moreover highlight the macroscopic Maxwell structure of the equations.

In essence, the strong formulation of the quasi-neutrality equation is the equation which, once multiplied by the scalar test function
\Lambda
and integrated over the spatial domain, results in the quasi-neutrality equation (
4.33
) after partial integration.
Here we note that the right-hand side of the quasi-neutrality equation (
4.33
) contains the gyro-average of the test function.
Hence, to find the strong formulation, we must define the gyro-average adjoint of the free charge density
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{f}}
, which is defined such that

\int\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}{\mathring{\Lambda}}\,\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z

(4.44a)

for all suitable test functions
\Lambda
.
Here we have omitted the application of the gyro-average operator on the test function as the gyro-averaging is already taken care of by the integration over
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u_{\shortparallel}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta
.
A similar definition for the gyro-average adjoint of the free current density holds

\displaystyle\int\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\bcdot{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\mathrel{\mathop{:}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\int f_{s}\Bigl(q_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}}\bcdot\left[{\bm{\Lambda}}+\nabla\langle\!|\rho\mathring{\Lambda}^{\varsigma}_{\rho}|\!\rangle+\langle\!|(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}})\times{\bm{\rho}}|\!\rangle\right]

\displaystyle-\mu\langle\!\langle(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}})_{\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\Bigr)\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z

(4.44b)

for which

\int\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\bcdot{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}{{\bm{U}}^{\star}\bcdot\mathring{{\bm{\Lambda}}}}\,\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B})

(4.45)

by making use of Eqs. (
D.10
) and (
D.7
).
Here we have defined the effective gyrocentre velocity as (cf.
Eq.

4.18
)

{\bm{U}}^{\star}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\dot{{\bm{R}}}+u_{\tau}{\bm{\hat{\tau}}}

(4.46)

for which
\langle{\bm{U}}^{\star}\rangle=\dot{{\bm{R}}}
.

The tangential velocity component
u_{\tau}
used in
Eq.

4.46
is in gyrocentre coordinates, i.e. it is defined according to
Eq.

2.12
evaluated at gyrocentre coordinates.
Similarly, the gyroradius
\rho
is defined according to
Eq.

2.20
evaluated at gyrocentre coordinates.

The contribution to the free current given by
-\mu\langle\!\langle(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}})_{\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle

results in the intrinsic guiding-centre magnetic moment and was included in
Eq.

4.40a
in order to define the rest-frame magnetic moment.
The complicated term that multiplies
\dot{{\bm{R}}}
on the right-hand side of
Eq.

4.44b
is essential in
Section

4.6
for showing that the field equations are compatible.
The key property of this term is found by letting
{\bm{\Lambda}}=\nabla\Lambda
, as one does when computing the divergence of the free current density adjoint.
This results in

\int\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\bcdot\nabla\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}}\bcdot\nabla\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z,

(4.47)

where we have made use of the gradient theorem (
D.8
) and shows that the gradient of the gyro-average of the test function is found, rather than the gyro-average of the gradient.
This equality is essential in showing that the gyro-average adjoint of the continuity equation also holds

\int\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{f}}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{f}}\right)\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r=0\quad\iff\quad\int\left(\frac{\partial\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoi 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\m 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\right)\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r=0,

(4.48)

as follows from multiplying
Eq.

4.10
by the gyro-averaged scalar test function
\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle
, integrating over the spatial domain, by making use of partial integration, and by substituting the gyro-average adjoints defined in
Eq.

4.44
.

We can write the strong formulation of our field equations as

\displaystyle\nabla\times{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}}{\partial t}+\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}},

(4.49a)

\displaystyle\nabla\times{\bm{E}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\partial{\bm{B}}}{\partial t},

(4.49b)

\displaystyle\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}},

(4.49c)

\displaystyle\nabla\bcdot{\bm{B}}

\displaystyle=0,

(4.49d)

where we have defined the displacement and magnetizing field as

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s},

(4.49e)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}{\bm{B}}-\sum_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}.

(4.49f)

The displacement current is given by
\partial{{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}}/\partial t
.
We recall that the polarization
{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}
and magnetization
{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}
are defined in
Eqs.

4.34
and
4.39
, respectively, and we note that
{\bm{B}}={\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1}
as well as
{\bm{E}}={\bm{E}}_{1}
.
Besides the quasi-neutrality equation (
4.49c
) and Ampère’s law (
4.49a
), we have additionally included Faraday’s equation (
4.49b
) as well as the magnetic Gauss law (
4.49d
).
The latter two equations are satisfied automatically when a potential formulation is used, but due to the gauge invariance of our model, we are able to express our model entirely in terms of the electromagnetic fields, which thereby requires
Eqs.

4.49b
and
4.49d
.

Writing the field equations in this way shows that the proposed gauge invariant gyrokinetic model can in fact be interpreted as a material property in the macroscopic Maxwell’s equations, when the quasi-neutral limit is considered.
As with Maxwell’s equations, we find that the field equations are compatible in the sense that the divergence of Ampère’s law equals the time derivative of the quasi-neutrality equation, by making use of the continuity equation (
4.10
).
This is a direct consequence of gauge invariance, as will be discussed in
Section

4.6
.

Due to the absence of the electric field contribution
\epsilon_{0}{\bm{E}}_{1}
in the displacement field, we find that the displacement field is in fact perpendicular to the background magnetic field
{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}\perp{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}
(by substituting
Eqs.

4.34
and
3.51
)

{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}=\sum_{s}\frac{m_{s}}{B_{0}^{2}}\int f^{0}_{s}\left({\bm{E}}_{1,\perp}+u_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{B}}_{1}\right)\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,

(4.50)

and therefore
Eq.

4.49a
yields an evolution equation for
{\bm{E}}_{1,\perp}
only and not for
E_{1,\shortparallel}
.
This means that, upon discretizing
Eq.

4.49
in space, we find a differential algebraic system of equations (DAEs), rather than a system of ordinary differential equations (ODEs).

4.5
Equilibrium solutions of the field equations

The gyrocentre coordinate transformation discussed in
Section

3
is based on the
assumption
that
\varepsilon_{\delta}\ll 1
, which we have not yet justified.
In order for this assumption to hold, we require that our initial particle distribution function
f^{0}_{s}
and background magnetic field
{\bm{B}}_{0}
are close to equilibrium.
That is, at
t=t^{0}
we require that the field equations approximately hold true to leading order in
\varepsilon_{\delta}
, i.e. when setting the perturbed fields to zero.
This results in equilibrium solutions of the field equations.

We assume that our background distribution function is nearly symmetric in
u_{\shortparallel}
, that is, it is of the form

f^{0}_{s}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel},\mu)=f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel}-\delta u_{s},\mu)\quad\text{with}\quad f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}({\bm{r}},u_{\shortparallel},\mu)=f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}({\bm{r}},-u_{\shortparallel},\mu),

(4.51)

where
\varepsilon_{U,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\delta u_{s}/u_{\mathrm{th},s}
is assumed to be small.
This results in

\int f^{0}_{s}u_{\shortparallel}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u=\int f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}\delta u_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u=\mathcal{N}_{00,s}\delta u_{s}.

(4.52)

Throughout the discussion on the equilibrium solutions we neglect
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B,s}^{2})
and
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B,s}\varepsilon_{U,s})
terms, and we consider the ZLR limit.

For the quasi-neutrality equation (
4.49c
) we find that the leading order part is given by

0=\sum_{s}q_{s}\mathcal{N}_{00,s},

(4.53)

where we have made use of
Eq.

4.11a
and have defined the background particle density as

\mathcal{N}_{00,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int f^{0}_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u.

(4.54)

The background distributions must result in an (approximately) quasi-neutral plasma in order to justify
\varepsilon_{\delta}\ll 1
.

The leading order part of the ZLR limit of Ampère’s law (
4.49a
) is given by

\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\nabla\times{\bm{B}}_{0}=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\mathrm{ZLR}}_{0},

(4.55)

where the ZLR limit of the background gyrocentre free current results in

\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\mathrm{ZLR}}_{0}=\sum_{s}q_{s}\mathcal{N}_{00,s}\delta u_{s}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+\frac{p_{0,\shortparallel}}{B_{0}}\nabla\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+\frac{p_{0,\perp}}{B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\nabla B_{0}-\nabla\times\left(\frac{p_{0,\perp}}{B_{0}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\right)

(4.56)

by making use of
Eqs.

4.44b
and
2.42a
.
The background pressures are defined as

\displaystyle p_{0,\shortparallel}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}m_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,

(4.57a)

\displaystyle p_{0,\perp}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}\frac{m_{s}}{2}\int f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}u_{\tau}^{2}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u=B_{0}\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}\mu\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u

(4.57b)

by making use of
Eq.

4.52
as well as

\displaystyle\int f^{0}_{s}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u

\displaystyle=\int f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{U,s}^{2}),

(4.58a)

\displaystyle\int f^{0}_{s}\mu\mathfrak{J}_{0,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u

\displaystyle=\int f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}\mu\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B,s}\varepsilon_{U,s}).

(4.58b)

We find that the perpendicular part of the ZLR limit of the background gyrocentre free current can alternatively be written as

\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\mathrm{ZLR}}_{0,\perp}=\frac{1}{B_{0}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times(\nabla\bcdot\mathsfbi{P}_{0}),\quad\mathsfbi{P}_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=p_{0,\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\otimes{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+p_{0,\perp}(\mathsfbi{I}_{3}-{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\otimes{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}),

(4.59)

where we have made use of

Q(\nabla\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})_{\perp}={\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\nabla\bcdot(Q{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\otimes{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}).

(4.60)

When combined with Ampère’s law this results in the equilibrium condition

(\nabla\times{\bm{B}}_{0})\times{\bm{B}}_{0}=\mu_{0}(\nabla\bcdot\mathsfbi{P}_{0})_{\perp},

(4.61)

which, for an isotropic background distribution with
p_{0}=p_{0,\shortparallel}=p_{0,\perp}
, results in the MHD equilibrium condition

(\nabla\times{\bm{B}}_{0})\times{\bm{B}}_{0}=\mu_{0}\nabla p_{0},

(4.62)

wherein we have imposed
{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla p_{0}=0
, as usually required by the tools for computing MHD equilibria.
The condition on the parallel derivative of the background pressure implies that the background particle density and temperature must be a function of the flux surface label.

MHD equilibria can be computed using software tools such as VMEC
(
Hirshman & Whitson 1983
)
and GVEC
(
Hindenlang et al. 2019
)
, which for a given geometry and pressure find a magnetic field
{\bm{B}}_{0}
such that
Eq.

4.62
holds.
However, we note that
Eqs.

4.62
and
4.61
only ensure that the perpendicular part of Ampère’s law is satisfied, and to this end we consider the parallel component of Ampère’s law

\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot(\nabla\times{\bm{B}}_{0})=\sum_{s}q_{s}\mathcal{N}_{00,s}\delta u_{s}+\frac{p_{0,\shortparallel}-p_{0,\perp}}{B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot(\nabla\times{\bm{B}}_{0}),

(4.63)

as follows substitution of the parallel component of
Eq.

4.56
.
Note that the shift
\delta u_{s}({\bm{R}})
(which may be a function of the gyrocentre position) is the only unknown in
Eq.

4.63
, and therefore this equation yields a constraint on the shift.
Moreover, it shows that the background distribution function must be strongly anisotropic if it is unshifted, unless
{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot(\nabla\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})=0
.
The smallness of the nondimensional shift
\varepsilon_{U,s}
per species can be deduced from the nondimensionalization of
Eq.

4.63

\mathcal{O}(1)=\sum_{s}\frac{\beta_{0,s}}{1-\frac{\mu_{0}(p_{0,\shortparallel}-p_{0,\perp})}{B_{0}^{2}}}\frac{\varepsilon_{U,s}}{\varepsilon_{B,s}},

(4.64)

where the plasma-
\beta
is defined per species as

\beta_{0,s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{2\mu_{0}\mathcal{N}_{00,s}k_{\mathrm{B}}T_{s}}{B_{0}^{2}}.

(4.65)

To summarize, we can choose any symmetric background distribution function
f^{0}_{s}{}^{,\mathrm{S}}
from which we compute the parallel and perpendicular pressure according to
Eq.

4.57
.
We then solve the equilibrium equation (
4.61
) (or
Eq.

4.62
if the background distribution function is isotropic), resulting in the background magnetic field
{\bm{B}}_{0}
.
Finally, we use
Eq.

4.63
to find the shifts
\delta u_{s}({\bm{R}})
, thereby adjusting the background distribution function
f^{0}_{s}
according to
Eq.

4.51
.
Constructing the background distribution function and magnetic field in this way ensures that we are near equilibrium, which thereby ensures that the underlying assumption of the gyrocentre coordinate transformation
\varepsilon_{\delta}\ll 1
is justified.

4.6
Well-posedness of the field equations

Well-posedness of the field equations is non-trivial.
With well-posedness we refer to the existence and uniqueness of solutions to the field equations.
This is a rather mathematical topic and therefore falls outside the scope of this paper when it is considered fully rigorously.
However, as we will see, a necessary condition for well-posedness is related to the bound charge continuity equation and thereby allows for a physical interpretation.

To illustrate that well-posedness is a non-trivial property, we consider the following general form of Ampère’s law and the quasi-neutrality equation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\nabla\times{\bm{B}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}+{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b}},

(4.66a)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoi
