455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}+\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{b}},

(4.66b)

where we have defined the bound charge as

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}

(4.67)

and consider some unspecified bound current denoted by
{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b}}
.
Computing the divergence of Ampère’s law then results in

0=\nabla\bcdot\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}+\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b}}=-\frac{\partial\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b}}\quad\iff\quad\underbrace{\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{b}}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b}}=0}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{bound charge}\\
\text{continuity equation}\end{subarray}},

(4.68)

where we have substituted the continuity equation (
4.48
) as well as the quasi-neutrality equation (
4.66b
).
That is, we find that the bound charge continuity equation (
4.68
) must hold for the two field equations to be compatible.
Note that compatibility here means that the divergence of Ampère’s law should coincide with the time derivative of the quasi-neutrality equation.
For our strong formulation we find

{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\partial{\bm{\mathcal{D}}}}{\partial t}+\sum_{s}\nabla\times{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}

(4.69)

for which it can easily be shown that
Eq.

4.68
is satisfied.

Remark 2

Compatibility of the field equations can also directly be deduced from the action principle by computing the following gauge invariant variation

\left.\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\varepsilon}\right|_{\varepsilon=0}\mathfrak{A}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1}-\varepsilon\partial\eta/\partial t,{\bm{A}}_{1}+\varepsilon\nabla\eta)=0

(4.70)

for some scalar test function
\eta
.
When substituting
Eq.

4.30
we find that this results in

0=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})\left(\nabla\eta\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}+\frac{\partial\eta}{\partial t}\right)\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(4.71)

which, by making use of
Theorem

2
as well as partial integration, results in

0=-\int\underbrace{\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{f}}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{f}}\right)}_{\overset{\eqref{eq:gy_continuity}}{=}0}\eta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(4.72)

where we have moreover substituted the definitions of free charge and current density as given by
Eq.

4.11
and thereby recognize the continuity equation (
4.10
).
It follows that this gauge invariant variation does not give an additional constraint, and we note that this specific variation coincides with computing the difference between the divergence of Ampère’s law (i.e. letting
{\bm{\Lambda}}=\nabla\eta
in
Eq.

4.38
) and the time derivative of the quasi-neutrality equation (i.e. letting
\Lambda=\partial\eta/\partial t
in
Eq.

4.33
).

4.7
Energy conservation

As our model is derived from an action principle, we can utilize Noether’s theorem
(
Noether 1918
)
in order to find the conserved energy
\mathfrak{E}
.
However, some intermediate results can be obtained as well.
We first consider the evolution of the kinetic energy per particle
K_{s}
, then we consider the evolution of the kinetic energy density
\mathcal{K}
and potential energy density
\mathcal{U}
, which finally leads to the conserved total energy
\mathfrak{E}
.

The kinetic energy per particle is defined as

K_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{s}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}}{2}+\mu(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle),

(4.73)

which is derived from applying our gyrocentre coordinate transformation to the guiding-centre kinetic energy
\bar{H}_{0}
(as defined in
Eq.

2.21
), i.e. it is defined using the effective magnetic moment from
Eq.

4.19
.
The kinetic energy of a particle evolves as

\frac{\mathrm{d}K_{s}}{\mathrm{d}t}=q_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}}\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}-M\langle\!\langle(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{E}}_{1})_{\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle

(4.74)

by making use of
Eq.

4.28
as well as Faraday’s equation (
4.25
).
Hence, if the perturbed electric field vanishes, we find that the kinetic energy per particle is conserved, as can be expected because magnetic fields do no work.

The kinetic energy per particle can be integrated over all particles resulting in the kinetic energy density

\mathcal{K}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}\int f_{s}K_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,

(4.75a)

whereas the displacement and magnetizing field result in the potential energy density

\mathcal{U}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{1}{2}({\bm{\mathcal{D}}}\bcdot{\bm{E}}+{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}\bcdot{\bm{B}}).

(4.75b)

This results in the following local energy conservation law for which a proof can be found in
Appendix

H
.

Theorem 3
(Local energy conservation)

The kinetic energy density (
4.75a
) satisfies

\frac{\partial\mathcal{K}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot\left(\sum_{s}\int f_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}}K_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u\right)=\hbox{\hskip 446.00386pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-446.00386pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-446.00386pt\hskip 446.00386pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\bcdot{\bm{E}},

(4.76)

whereas the potential energy density (
4.75b
) satisfies Poynting’s theorem

\frac{\partial\mathcal{U}}{\partial t}+\nabla\bcdot({\bm{E}}\times{\bm{\mathcal{H}}})=-\hbox{\hskip 446.00386pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-446.00386pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-446.00386pt\hskip 446.00386pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}\bcdot{\bm{E}}.

(4.77)

The magnetizing field
{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}
and free current density
\hbox{\hskip 446.00386pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-446.00386pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-446.00386pt\hskip 446.00386pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}
are defined in Eqs. (
4.49f
) and (
4.44b
), respectively.
It follows that the following local energy conservation law holds

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{U})+\nabla\bcdot\left({\bm{E}}\times{\bm{\mathcal{H}}}+\sum_{s}\int f_{s}\dot{{\bm{R}}}K_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u\right)=0.

(4.78)

When integrating the sum of the kinetic and potential energies over the spatial domain we find the total energy

\mathfrak{E}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{U})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r,

(4.79)

which is conserved as a consequence of
Eq.

4.78
.

Remark 3

The derivation of the total energy presented above is based on the strong formulation of the equations, but we note that the conserved energy can also be derived directly from the field Lagrangian by considering the total time derivative of the field Lagrangian (
4.16
)

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{L}}{\mathrm{d}t}=\frac{\delta\mathfrak{L}}{\delta{\bm{Z}}}\left[\dot{{\bm{Z}}}\right]+\frac{\delta\mathfrak{L}}{\delta\phi_{1}}\left[\frac{\partial\phi_{1}}{\partial t}\right]+\frac{\delta\mathfrak{L}}{\delta{\bm{A}}_{1}}\left[\frac{\partial{\bm{A}}_{1}}{\partial t}\right].

(4.80)

We then assume that the characteristics and fields satisfy the EOMs and field equations, respectively, and make use of the fact that these equations are found by setting the respective variation of the action to zero, up to partial integration in time.
For instance, for the gyrocentre characteristics we find that

\frac{\delta\mathfrak{L}}{\delta{\bm{Z}}}\left[\dot{{\bm{Z}}}\right]=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0}){\frac{\partial L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}}{\partial\dot{{\bm{Z}}}}\bcdot\dot{{\bm{Z}}}}\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}

(4.81)

by making use of
Eq.

4.22
.
Similarly, we find that
Remark

1
becomes

\frac{\delta\mathfrak{L}}{\delta{\bm{A}}_{1}}\left[\frac{\partial{\bm{A}}_{1}}{\partial t}\right]=-\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\sum_{s}\int\left(q_{s}f_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{s}\rho\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\langle\!|\mathring{A}^{\varsigma}_{1,\rho}|\!\rangle+f^{0}_{s}{\bm{P}}_{1,s}\bcdot\frac{\partial{\bm{A}}_{1}}{\partial t}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z

(4.82)

upon substitution of Ampère’s law.
The variation for
\phi_{1}
did not require any partial integration in time, and therefore we find that
Eq.

4.80
results in

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{E}}{\mathrm{d}t}=0,

(4.83)

where the conserved energy is given by

\mathfrak{E}=\sum_{s}\int\left(f_{s}{\frac{\partial L_{s}^{\mathrm{part}}}{\partial\dot{{\bm{Z}}}}\bcdot\dot{{\bm{Z}}}}\mathfrak{J}_{s}-q_{s}f_{s}\rho\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\langle\!|\mathring{A}^{\varsigma}_{1,\rho}|\!\rangle\mathfrak{J}_{s}-f^{0}_{s}\frac{m_{s}}{q_{s}B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{F}}_{1,\perp}\bcdot\frac{\partial{\bm{A}}_{1}}{\partial t}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z-\mathfrak{L}.

(4.84)

It can be shown that
Eq.

4.84
reduces to
Eq.

4.79
by substituting the definitions of the particle (
4.17b
) and field (
4.17c
) Hamiltonian in Low’s action (
4.16
), and by making use of the quasi-neutrality equation as well as the gradient theorem (
D.8
).

5
Comparison with reduced models and models from literature

The gyrokinetic model which has been derived thus far is more comprehensive and possesses more structure than the models usually found in literature
(
Kleiber et al. 2016
;
Brizard & Hahm 2007
;
Qin et al. 1999
)
.
Typically, such models are reduced ‘parallel-only’ models wherein it is assumed that the perpendicular part of the vector potential can be neglected.
In this section, we compare our gyrokinetic model to several models from the literature.
Additionally, a gyrokinetic Darwin approximation of our proposed gauge invariant model is considered, in which the fast compressional Alfvén wave is eliminated, while preserving the structure of our field equations.

For each model under consideration, we discuss the Hamiltonian, the resulting EOMs and field equations as well as their corresponding strong form.
Furthermore, the well-posedness of the models is discussed and dispersion relations are derived and used to compare the models in terms of the presence of shear and/or compressional Alfvén waves.

5.1
Parallel-only model

The parallel-only model, as is discussed for example in
(
Kleiber et al. 2016
)
, is based on the assumption that the perpendicular part of the vector potential can be neglected:
{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}={\bm{0}}_{3}
.
This assumption is combined with the following approximation

{\bm{B}}_{1}=\nabla\times(A_{1,\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})\approx\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0},

(5.1)

wherein the
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B})
contribution to
{\bm{B}}_{1}
has been neglected.

5.1.1
The Hamiltonian

When neglecting the perpendicular part of the vector potential and by making use of the approximation given by
Eq.

5.1
, we find that the first- and second-order Hamiltonian are reduced to (cf.
Eq.

3.47
)

H_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=q_{s}\langle\mathring{\phi}_{1}\rangle

(5.2a)

and (cf.
Eq.

3.57
)

H_{2,s}^{\shortparallel}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-\frac{m_{s}}{2B_{0}^{2}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}(\phi_{1}-U_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel})\rvert^{2}+\frac{M}{2B_{0}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\rvert^{2},

(5.2b)

respectively, by making use of the gradient theorem (
D.8
).

5.1.2
Principle of least action

We find that the reduced EOMs are given by

\displaystyle\dot{{\bm{R}}}

\displaystyle=U_{\shortparallel}{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}-\frac{1}{q_{s}B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\left(q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\shortparallel}-M\nabla B_{0}\right),

(5.3a)

\displaystyle\dot{U}_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{s}}{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}\bcdot\left(q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\shortparallel}-M\nabla B_{0}\right),

(5.3b)

where the reduced FLR corrected electric field is defined as (cf.
Eq.

4.24
)

{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\shortparallel}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-\nabla\langle\mathring{\phi}_{1}\rangle-\frac{\partial\langle\mathring{A}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle}{\partial t}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}.

(5.4)

Note that the FLR corrected electromagnetic fields now coincide exactly with the appropriately gyro-averaged electromagnetic fields, since the reduced model neglects exactly the
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B})
terms still present in
Eqs.

4.24
and
3.59
.

For the derivation of the field equations we again give Low’s action explicitly resulting in (cf.
Eq.

4.30
)

\mathfrak{A}^{\shortparallel}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},A_{1,\shortparallel})=\sum_{s}\int f_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\biggl[q_{s}\left({\bm{A}}_{0,s}^{\star}+\langle\mathring{A}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\right)\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}+\frac{m_{s}M}{q_{s}}\dot{\Theta}-MB_{0}-\frac{m_{s}}{2}U_{\shortparallel}^{2}\\
-q_{s}\langle\mathring{\phi}_{1}\rangle\biggr]\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}\biggl(\frac{m_{s}}{2B_{0}^{2}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}(\phi_{1}-u_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel})\rvert^{2}-\frac{\mu}{2B_{0}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\rvert^{2}\biggr)\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t\\
-\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}\int\lvert{\bm{B}}_{0}+\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t.

(5.5)

The quasi-neutrality equation (cf.
Eq.

4.33
) and Ampère’s law (cf.
Eq.

4.38
) are given by

\displaystyle-\sum_{s}\int{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z,

(5.6a)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\int\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x

\displaystyle=\sum_{s}\int{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\bcdot(\nabla_{\perp}\Lambda\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r+\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\dot{R}_{\shortparallel}\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z,

(5.6b)

where the reduced polarization (cf.
Eq.

4.34
) and magnetization (cf.
Eq.

4.39
) are given by

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int f^{0}_{s}{\bm{P}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,\quad{\bm{P}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{s}}{q_{s}B_{0}^{2}}{\bm{F}}_{1,\perp}^{\shortparallel},\quad{\bm{F}}_{1,\perp}^{\shortparallel}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-q_{s}\nabla_{\perp}(\phi_{1}-u_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel}),

(5.7a)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int f^{0}_{s}{\bm{M}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u,\quad{\bm{M}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\left(u_{\shortparallel}{\bm{P}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}-\frac{\mu}{B_{0}}\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\right).

(5.7b)

When considering the EOMs (
5.3
) as well as field equations (
5.6
) of the reduced model, we find that this model coincides with reduced parallel models from the literature.
We note that this is only due to the choice
(\xi_{R},\xi_{\Theta})=(1,0)
, which yields the appropriate gyro-averages on both the scalar and vector potential.

5.1.3
Strong formulation

In order to be able to interpret the equations more easily, we present the strong formulation of the field equations as follows

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\nabla\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}+\nabla\bcdot\left[\mathcal{C}(u_{\shortparallel})\nabla_{\perp}\phi_{1}+\frac{p_{0,\perp}-p_{0,\shortparallel}}{B_{0}^{2}}\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\right]

\displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{$\mathcal{J}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\shortparallel}_{\shortparallel},

(5.8a)

\displaystyle-\nabla\bcdot\left[\mathcal{C}(1)\nabla_{\perp}\phi_{1}-\mathcal{C}(u_{\shortparallel})\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\right]

\displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}},

(5.8b)

where we have defined the spatially varying functions
\mathcal{C}(\zeta)
as

\mathcal{C}(\zeta)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}\frac{m_{s}}{B_{0}^{2}}\int f^{0}_{s}\zeta\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u.

(5.9)

The gyro-average adjoint of the parallel component of the free current
\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\mathcal{J}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\shortparallel}_{\shortparallel}
is different from the one discussed in
Section

4.4
, and it is defined in a weak sense as (cf.
Eq.

4.44b
)

\int\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\mathcal{J}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\shortparallel}_{\shortparallel}\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sum_{s}q_{s}\int f_{s}\dot{{R}}_{\shortparallel}\langle\mathring{\Lambda}\rangle\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z.

(5.10)

The field equations (
5.8
) become decoupled when a centred Maxwellian background particle distribution function is considered, which is defined as

f^{0,\mathrm{CM}}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{00,s}}{\sqrt{\pi}^{3}u_{\mathrm{th},s}^{3}}\exp{\left(-\frac{m_{s}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}+2B_{0}\mu}{m_{s}u_{\mathrm{th},s}^{2}}\right)}.

(5.11)

The following identities, which hold for a centred Maxwellian background distribution, result in significant simplifications

\int f^{0}_{s}u_{\shortparallel}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u=0,\quad\int f^{0}_{s}\left(m_{s}u_{\shortparallel}^{2}-\mu B_{0}\right)\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}u=0.

(5.12)

Note that
Eq.

5.12
, in physical terms, coincides with the absence of a parallel background current as well as the isotropy of the temperature.
In particular, this results in symmetry
\mathcal{C}(u_{\shortparallel})=0
and isotropy
p_{0,\perp}=p_{0,\shortparallel}
, thereby fully decoupling the field equations (
5.8
).

5.2
Symplectic gyrokinetic model from Brizard–Hahm

Besides the gauge invariant model described in
(
Burby & Brizard 2019
)
, there are also gauge variant gyrokinetic models which include
{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}
.
In particular, we consider the symplectic model from
(
Brizard & Hahm 2007
, Eqs. (171) and (173) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(1,1)
)
, which we hereafter refer to as the Brizard–Hahm (BH) model.
In our terminology, this model coincides with the choice of gyrocentre coordinates given by
Eq.

3.36
.

5.2.1
The Hamiltonian

The first- and second-order Hamiltonian are derived from
(
Brizard & Hahm 2007
, Eqs. (171) and (173) with
(\alpha,\beta)=(1,1)
)
resulting in (cf.
Eqs.

3.47
and
5.2a
)

H_{1,s}^{\mathrm{BH}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=q_{s}\langle\mathring{\phi}_{1}\rangle+M\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle

(5.13a)

and (cf.
Eqs.

3.57
and
5.2b
)

H_{2,s}^{\mathrm{BH}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-\frac{m_{s}}{2B_{0}^{2}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}(\phi_{1}-U_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel})\rvert^{2}+\frac{M}{2B_{0}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\rvert^{2},

(5.13b)

respectively.
The derivation, which neglects terms of
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{3})
in
H_{2,s}^{\mathrm{BH}}
, can be found in
Appendix

I
.
We note that this result can alternatively be derived from
Eq.

3.57
with
\xi_{R}=1
, when making use of the approximation
{\bm{B}}_{1}\approx\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}
and neglecting
{\partial{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}}/{\partial t}
.

Note that the second-order Hamiltonian coincides exactly with that of the parallel-only model:
H_{2,s}^{\mathrm{BH}}=H_{2,s}^{\shortparallel}
.
This is remarkable, as it implies an absence of the polarization current as well as an absence of a contribution to the magnetization from the perpendicular part of the vector potential.

Both our proposed gauge invariant model and the Brizard–Hahm model
(
Brizard & Hahm 2007
)
reduce to the parallel-only model when neglecting the perpendicular part of the vector potential.
Note that the same cannot be said about the gauge invariant model from
(
Burby & Brizard 2019
)
, which coincides with
(\xi_{R},\xi_{\Theta})=(0,0)
.
Therein, we find that, e.g.
{\bm{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}={\bm{A}}_{1}
such that the vector potential without gyro-averaging appears in the EOMs.

5.2.2
Principle of least action

The EOMs are found by substituting the expression for the particle Hamiltonian
H^{\mathrm{part},\mathrm{BH}}=H_{0}+H_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}
into the general form of the EOMs given by
Eq.

3.69
, where we now have
{\bm{\mathcal{A}}}_{1}=\langle\mathring{{\bm{A}}}_{1}\rangle
, which therefore also changes the definition of the effective gyrocentre magnetic field
{\bm{B}}_{s}^{\star}
given by
Eq.

3.62
.
This results in (cf.
Eqs.

4.28
and
5.3
)

\displaystyle\dot{{\bm{R}}}

\displaystyle=U_{\shortparallel}{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}-\frac{1}{q_{s}B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\left[q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}-M\nabla\left(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\right)\right],

(5.14a)

\displaystyle\dot{U}_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m_{s}}{\bm{b}}_{s}^{\star}\bcdot\left[q_{s}{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}-M\nabla\left(B_{0}+\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\right)\right],

(5.14b)

where the reduced FLR corrected electric field is defined as (cf.
Eqs.

4.24
and
5.4
)

{\bm{\mathcal{E}}}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=-\nabla\langle\mathring{\phi}_{1}\rangle-\frac{\partial\langle\mathring{{\bm{A}}}_{1}\rangle}{\partial t}.

(5.15)

Note that
the EOMs given by
Eq.

5.14
coincide with the ones found in
Eq.

4.28
when
\varepsilon_{B}=0
.

The action is explicitly given by

\mathfrak{A}^{\mathrm{BH}}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1},\lambda)=\sum_{s}\int f_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0},t^{0})\biggl[q_{s}\left({\bm{A}}_{0,s}^{\star}+\langle\mathring{{\bm{A}}}_{1}\rangle\right)\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}+\frac{m_{s}M}{q_{s}}\dot{\Theta}-MB_{0}-\frac{m_{s}}{2}U_{\shortparallel}^{2}\\
-q_{s}\langle\mathring{\phi}_{1}\rangle-M\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\biggr]\mathfrak{J}_{s}({\bm{z}}^{0})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z^{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{s}\int f^{0}_{s}\biggl(\frac{m_{s}}{2B_{0}^{2}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}(\phi_{1}-u_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel})\rvert^{2}\\
-\frac{\mu}{2B_{0}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\rvert^{2}\biggr)\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t-\frac{1}{2\mu_{0}}\int\lvert{\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t+\int C^{\mathrm{BH}}({\bm{Z}},\phi_{1},{\bm{A}}_{1},\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t,

(5.16)

where a Lagrange multiplier
\lambda
is introduced, with an associated constraint
C^{\mathrm{BH}}
, to ensure that a well-posed system of equations is found, as will be discussed in more detail in
Section

5.2.4
.
Therein, we will show that the constraint necessarily depends on all unknowns of our problem, including the characteristics
{\bm{Z}}
.
Hence, when using a variational formulation of the Brizard–Hahm model
(
Brizard & Hahm 2007
)
we find that well-posedness of the model implies that the Lagrange multiplier
\lambda
affects the EOMs as well as each of the field equations.
We do not show this dependence here explicitly, and for now we ignore the contribution due to the constraint.

The action (
5.16
) results in the same quasi-neutrality equation as found for the reduced parallel-only model, as given by
Eq.

5.6a
.
We find that Ampère’s law is now given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\int({\bm{B}}_{0}+{\bm{B}}_{1})\bcdot\nabla\times{\bm{\Lambda}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x={}

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\int\nabla\bcdot({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})\Lambda_{\shortparallel}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r

\displaystyle+\sum_{s}\int f_{s}\left[q_{s}\langle\mathring{{\bm{\Lambda}}}\rangle\bcdot\dot{{\bm{R}}}-\mu\langle\!\langle(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}}_{\perp})_{\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\right]\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z.

(5.17)

5.2.3
Strong formulation

As usual, we consider the strong formulation of the field equations, this results in the following Ampère’s law and quasi-neutrality equation (cf.
Eq.

4.49
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\nabla\times{\bm{B}}

\displaystyle=\overbrace{\sum_{s}\left[{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\nabla\bcdot({\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})\right]}^{{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{BH}}={\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{m},\mathrm{BH}}}+\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f},\mathrm{BH}},

(5.18a)

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.68056pt\hbox{$\mathcal{R}$}\hskip-3.68056pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoi 455.45732pt}{}^{\mathrm{f}}.

(5.18b)

The meaning of the gyro-average adjoint of the free current
\hbox{\hskip is different from the one discussed in

, and it is defined in a weak sense as (cf.

)

f_{s}\left[\dot{{\bm{R}}}\bcdot\langle\mathring{{\bm{\Lambda}}}\rangle-\mu\langle\!\langle(\mathring{\nabla}^{\varsigma}\times{\bm{\Lambda}})_{\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\right]\mathfrak{J}_{s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}z.

(5.19)

This free current adjoint coincides with the free current adjoint from our proposed model, as given by
Eq.

4.44b
, whenever
\varepsilon_{B}=0
thanks to
Eq.

D.10
.

Contrary to our proposed gauge invariant model discussed in strong formulation in
Section

4.4
, we now fail to recognize the structure of the macroscopic Maxwell’s equations in
Eq.

5.18
.
This is primarily due to the absence of a polarization current, but also due to the fact that the magnetization current
{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}^{\mathrm{m},\mathrm{BH}}
cannot be written as the curl of a magnetization.
Even when neglecting
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B})
contributions this is not possible, in which case we find

{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}{{}^{\mathrm{m,\mathrm{BH}}}}=(\nabla\times{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{1,s}^{\shortparallel})_{\shortparallel}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{B}).

(5.20)

In order to further simplify the field equations we assume that our background particle distribution function is given by a centred Maxwellian (
5.11
).
The resulting field equations are given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}\nabla\times({\bm{B}}_{0}+\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1})

(5.21a)

5.2.4
Well-posedness of the field equations

For the symplectic Brizard–Hahm model
(
Brizard & Hahm 2007
)
we note that the free current density is defined differently from the free current density of our proposed model (
4.44b
).
This implies, in particular, that the gyro-average of the free charge continuity equation is no longer satisfied

\int\left(\frac{\partial\hbox{\hskip
