\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\frac{B_{0}^{2}}{qm}\lvert{\bm{Q}}_{1}\rvert^{2},

(I.8)

where we made use of
Eq.

D.5b
.
The second contribution can trivially be evaluated as

\hbox to14.87pt{\vbox to14.87pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.43628pt\lower-7.43628pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.94 0 C 9.94 5.49 5.49 9.94 0 9.94 C -5.49 9.94 -9.94 5.49 -9.94 0 C -9.94 -5.49 -5.49 -9.94 0 -9.94 C 5.49 -9.94 9.94 -5.49 9.94 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.33896pt}{-1.73332pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\frac{2\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}[(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}]^{2},

(I.9)

whereas the third contribution yields

\hbox to14.87pt{\vbox to14.87pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.43628pt\lower-7.43628pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.94 0 C 9.94 5.49 5.49 9.94 0 9.94 C -5.49 9.94 -9.94 5.49 -9.94 0 C -9.94 -5.49 -5.49 -9.94 0 -9.94 C 5.49 -9.94 9.94 -5.49 9.94 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.33896pt}{-1.73332pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\frac{4\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}\left(\partial_{\hat{2}}A_{1,\hat{1}}-\partial_{\hat{1}}A_{1,\hat{2}}\right)=-\frac{4\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}^{2}

(I.10)

by making use of

\langle{\bm{\hat{\rho}}}\otimes{\bm{\hat{\tau}}}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}({\bm{\hat{e}}}_{2}\otimes{\bm{\hat{e}}}_{1}-{\bm{\hat{e}}}_{1}\otimes{\bm{\hat{e}}}_{2}).

(I.11)

The fourth contribution can be written as

\hbox to14.87pt{\vbox to14.87pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.43628pt\lower-7.43628pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.94 0 C 9.94 5.49 5.49 9.94 0 9.94 C -5.49 9.94 -9.94 5.49 -9.94 0 C -9.94 -5.49 -5.49 -9.94 0 -9.94 C 5.49 -9.94 9.94 -5.49 9.94 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.33896pt}{-1.73332pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}\left(\lvert\nabla_{\perp}{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}+2[(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}]^{2}-2\det\mathsfbi{G}_{\perp}\right),

(I.12)

where we make use of

\langle\sin^{4}\theta\rangle=\langle\cos^{4}\theta\rangle=\frac{3}{8},\quad\langle\sin^{2}\theta\cos^{2}\theta\rangle=\frac{1}{8}

(I.13)

and define

\mathsfbi{G}_{\perp}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\hat{1}}A_{1,\hat{1}}&\partial_{\hat{2}}A_{1,\hat{1}}\\
\partial_{\hat{1}}A_{1,\hat{2}}&\partial_{\hat{2}}A_{1,\hat{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

(I.14)

Finally, the fifth contribution can be written as

\hbox to14.87pt{\vbox to14.87pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 7.43628pt\lower-7.43628pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.94 0 C 9.94 5.49 5.49 9.94 0 9.94 C -5.49 9.94 -9.94 5.49 -9.94 0 C -9.94 -5.49 -5.49 -9.94 0 -9.94 C 5.49 -9.94 9.94 -5.49 9.94 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.33896pt}{-1.73332pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}\left[{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}-2({\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\times\nabla_{\perp}[(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}])_{\shortparallel}\right].

(I.15)

Since the
B
term in
Eq.

I.4
already has a factor
\varepsilon_{\perp}^{2}
, we need to only keep the zeroth order term of
\widetilde{\psi}_{1}
(see
Section

I.1
) for obtaining an
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{3})
approximation

\widetilde{\psi}_{1}=\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}A_{1,\tau}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp})

(I.16)

and similarly for
S_{1}
for the computation of the second term in
Eq.

I.4
resulting in

\hbox to10.05pt{\vbox to10.05pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-5.02734pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.61 0 C 6.61 3.65 3.65 6.61 0 6.61 C -3.65 6.61 -6.61 3.65 -6.61 0 C -6.61 -3.65 -3.65 -6.61 0 -6.61 C 3.65 -6.61 6.61 -3.65 6.61 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.00836pt}{-2.73332pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=-\frac{\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}}{B_{0}\omega_{\mathrm{c}}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\hat{1}}\times\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\hat{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{3})=-\frac{2\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}\det\mathsfbi{G}_{\perp}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{3}).

(I.17)

By making use of

\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}\lvert{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}=\lvert\nabla_{\perp}{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}+{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\bcdot\nabla_{\perp}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp},

(I.18)

we then find that the contribution to
\bar{\bar{H}}_{2}^{\mathrm{BH}}
due to the first-order generating function can be approximated by (neglecting
\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{3})
terms)

\langle\{{\bar{\bar{S}}}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}},\widetilde{\psi}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}\}_{0}\rangle=\frac{B_{0}^{2}}{qm}\lvert{\bm{Q}}_{1}\rvert^{2}+\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{qB_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}\lvert{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}-2{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\times\nabla\left[(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp})_{\shortparallel}\right]\right).

(I.19)

Note that in principle a high-frequency approximation of the first-order generating function
\bar{\bar{S}}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}
can also be considered, such as in the work of
(
Qin et al. 1999
)
.

I.2
ZLR approximation of the Hamiltonian

The remaining gyro-averaged terms in
\bar{\bar{H}}_{2}^{\mathrm{BH}}
can be approximated in the ZLR limit as follows

\displaystyle\frac{q^{2}}{2m}\langle(\widetilde{A}_{1,\shortparallel})^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{2B_{0}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{4}),

(I.20)

\displaystyle\frac{q^{2}}{2m}\langle\lvert\mathring{{\bm{A}}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{q^{2}}{2m}\lvert{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}+\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{4B_{0}}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}\lvert{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{4}),

(I.21)

\displaystyle\langle\mathring{{\bm{A}}}_{1,\perp}\rangle

\displaystyle={\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{4}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{4})

(I.22)

by making use of
Eq.

D.4
.
Moreover, we note that

\langle\mathring{\psi}_{1}^{\mathrm{BH}}\rangle=\phi_{1}-\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{4}\nabla_{\perp}^{2}(\phi_{1}-\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel})+\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{q}(\nabla\times{\bm{A}}_{1})_{\shortparallel}+\mathcal{O}(\varepsilon_{\perp}^{3}),

(I.23)

by making use of
Eqs.

D.7
and
D.4
.
Combining the ZLR approximations results in the following ZLR approximation of the second-order Hamiltonian

\bar{\bar{H}}_{2}^{\mathrm{BH}}=-\frac{B_{0}^{2}}{2m}\lvert{\bm{Q}}_{1}\rvert^{2}+\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{2B_{0}}\lvert\nabla_{\perp}A_{1,\shortparallel}\rvert^{2}+\frac{q^{2}}{2m}\lvert{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\rvert^{2}+\frac{q}{B_{0}}{\bm{A}}_{1,\perp}\bcdot[{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times\nabla_{\perp}(\phi_{1}-\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}A_{1,\shortparallel})],

(I.24)

which can be simplified to
Eq.

5.13b
.

Appendix J
Well-posedness of the saddle-point problem

Here we briefly, and rather informally, discuss the well-posedness of the saddle-point problem given by
Eq.

5.35
under the simplifying assumptions of a constant background magnetic field, an isotropic pressure
p_{0,\perp}=p_{0,\shortparallel}
as well as a spatially constant coefficient
\mathcal{C}(1)
.
Therefore, the following system of equations are considered

\displaystyle\nabla\times(\nabla\times{\bm{A}})-\nabla_{\perp}\lambda

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathcal{J}}},

(J.1a)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\perp}\bcdot{\bm{A}}

\displaystyle=0,

(J.1b)

where
\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}=0
and with appropriate boundary conditions.
Assume that
\lambda=0
and that
{\bm{A}}
satisfies

\displaystyle-\nabla_{\perp}^{2}A_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{J}_{\shortparallel},

(J.2a)

\displaystyle-\nabla^{2}{\bm{A}}_{\perp}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mathcal{J}}}_{\perp}-\nabla_{\perp}({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla A_{\shortparallel}),

(J.2b)

then we can show that
(\lambda,{\bm{A}})
also satisfy
Eq.

J.1
provided that
\varepsilon_{B}=0
.
Since each of the PDEs in
Eq.

J.2
is well-posed provided with appropriate boundary conditions, we find that
Eq.

J.1
is well-posed as well.

The equivalence can be shown as follows.
Assume that
{\bm{A}}
solves
Eq.

J.2
.
Computing the divergence of
Eq.

J.2b
results in

\nabla^{2}(\nabla_{\perp}\bcdot{\bm{A}})=\nabla_{\perp}\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}-{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla(\nabla_{\perp}^{2}A_{\shortparallel})=\nabla_{\perp}\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}+{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla(\mathcal{J}_{\shortparallel})=\nabla\bcdot{\bm{\mathcal{J}}}=0

(J.3)

upon substitution of
Eq.

J.2a
.
If homogenous Dirichlet boundary conditions are ‘included’ in the Laplace operator, then this implies that
Eq.

J.1b
holds.
Finally, we add
{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}
times
Eq.

J.2a
to
Eq.

J.2b
resulting in

{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla)^{2}A_{\shortparallel}-\nabla^{2}{\bm{A}}={\bm{\mathcal{J}}}-\nabla_{\perp}({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla A_{\shortparallel}).

(J.4)

When rearranging the terms and by making use of
Eq.

J.1b
, we find that

\nabla(\nabla\bcdot{\bm{A}})-\nabla^{2}{\bm{A}}={\bm{\mathcal{J}}},

(J.5)

which shows that
{\bm{A}}
satisfies
Eq.

J.1a
.

Appendix K
The susceptibility tensor in a slab

For the computation of the susceptibility tensor we require a linearization of our model under a Fourier ansatz.
In particular, we require the Fourier component of the linearized distribution function, the free current, the polarization current, and finally the magnetization current.
Once each of these Fourier components is known, we combine the results into the gyrokinetic susceptibility tensor, and we compute the drift kinetic limit.

K.1
The Fourier component of the linearized distribution function

We need a closed-form expression for
\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}
, which can be obtained upon substitution of the Fourier ansatz

\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}=\widehat{\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}}(\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel},\bar{\bar{\mu}})\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}({\bm{k}}\bcdot\bar{\bar{{\bm{r}}}}-\omega t)}

(K.1)

into the linearized Vlasov equation (cf.
Eq.

4.3
)

\frac{\partial\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}}{\partial t}+\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\nabla\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}-\frac{1}{m_{s}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\left(\mu\nabla\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle-q_{s}\langle\mathring{{\bm{E}}}_{1}\rangle\right)\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\partial\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}=0,

(K.2)

where we have made use of

(B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}\dot{\bar{\bar{{\bm{R}}}}})_{0}={\bm{B}}_{0}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel},\quad(B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}\dot{\bar{\bar{{\bm{R}}}}})_{1}=\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}\langle\mathring{{\bm{B}}}_{1}\rangle+\left(\langle\mathring{{\bm{E}}}_{1}\rangle-\frac{\mu}{q_{s}}\nabla\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle\right)\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}

(K.3)

using the gyrocentre EOM (
4.29
) and
Eqs.

3.62
and
3.60

as well as

(B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}\dot{\bar{\bar{U}}}_{\shortparallel})_{0}=0,\quad(B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}\dot{\bar{\bar{U}}}_{\shortparallel})_{1}=-\frac{1}{m}{\bm{B}}_{0}\bcdot\left(\mu\nabla\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle-q_{s}\langle\mathring{{\bm{E}}}_{1}\rangle\right),

(K.4)

which follows from substituting
Eqs.

3.65
and
4.23
into the gyrocentre EOM (
3.69b
) and having assumed
\nabla\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}={\bm{0}}_{3}
.
Subsequent substitution of the Fourier ansatzes (
K.1
) and Eq. (
5.41
) (and similarly for the magnetic field) results in

-\mathrm{i}\omega\widehat{\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}}+\mathrm{i}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot{\bm{k}}\widehat{\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}}-\frac{1}{m_{s}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot\left(\mathrm{i}\mu{\bm{k}}\widehat{\langle\!\langle\mathring{B}^{\varsigma}_{1,\shortparallel}\rangle\!\rangle}-q_{s}\widehat{\langle\mathring{{\bm{E}}}_{1}\rangle}\right)\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\partial\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}=0.

(K.5)

In order to proceed, we must express the Fourier component of the gyro-average of a function in terms of the Fourier component of that function itself.
We write the wavevector
{\bm{k}}
in terms of its parallel and perpendicular components as

{\bm{k}}=k_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}+k_{\perp}({\bm{\hat{e}}}_{1}\cos\alpha+{\bm{\hat{e}}}_{2}\sin\alpha)

(K.6)

for some angle
\alpha
.
It follows that the gyro-average of a function
Q

Q({\bm{r}})=\int\widehat{Q}({\bm{k}})\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}{\bm{k}}\bcdot{\bm{r}}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}k

(K.7)

can be expressed in terms of the Fourier component in the following way

\langle\mathring{Q}\rangle({\bm{r}})=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int\widehat{Q}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}{\bm{k}}\bcdot{\bm{r}}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}\rho\cos\theta}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}k=\int\widehat{Q}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}{\bm{k}}\bcdot{\bm{r}}}J_{0}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}k

(K.8)

and therefore

\widehat{\langle\mathring{Q}\rangle}=J_{0}\widehat{Q},

(K.9)

where
J_{n}
denotes the
n
-th order Bessel function of the first kind evaluated at
k_{\perp}\rho

J_{n}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}^{n}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}\rho\cos\theta}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\theta n}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta.

(K.10)

A similar computation for the disc average,
Eq.

3.44
, yields

\widehat{\langle\!\langle\mathring{Q}^{\varsigma}\rangle\!\rangle}=2\int_{0}^{1}\varsigma J_{0}(\varsigma k_{\perp}\rho)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varsigma\,\widehat{Q}=\frac{2J_{1}}{k_{\perp}\rho}\widehat{Q}.

(K.11)

Substitution of
Eqs.

K.9
and
K.11
in
Eq.

K.5
yields

\widehat{\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}}={\bm{f}}_{s}\bcdot\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1},\quad{\bm{f}}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left(J_{0}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}-\mathrm{i}\frac{2\bar{\bar{\mu}}k_{\shortparallel}J_{1}}{\omega q_{s}k_{\perp}\rho}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}}\right)\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}\frac{\partial\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\partial\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}}{\mathrm{i}B_{0}(\omega-k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel})},

(K.12)

where we have made use of Faraday’s equation in Fourier space

\widehat{{\bm{B}}}_{1}=\frac{1}{\omega}{\bm{k}}\times\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}.

(K.13)

K.2
The Fourier component of the linearized gyrocentre free current

We write the gyrocentre current (
4.44b

We need the Fourier component of the adjoint of the gyro-average for evaluating
Eq.

K.16
.
The gyro-average adjoint on
\Omega=\mathbb{R}^{3}
, with constant
{\bm{B}}_{0}
, is given by

\int Q\Lambda(\bar{\bar{{\bm{r}}}}+\rho(\bar{\bar{\mu}}){\bm{\hat{\rho}}}(\bar{\bar{\theta}}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}\bar{\bar{z}}=\int Q(\bar{\bar{{\bm{r}}}}-\rho(\bar{\bar{\mu}}){\bm{\hat{\rho}}}(\bar{\bar{\theta}}))\Lambda\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{6}\bar{\bar{z}}\quad\implies\quad\langle\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt}\rangle=\langle Q(\bar{\bar{{\bm{r}}}}-{\bm{\rho}})\rangle,

(K.17)

where we have made use of the coordinate transformation

(K.19)

by the symmetry of the zeroth-order Bessel function of the first kind.
A similar result holds for the adjoint of the disc average

\langle\!\langle\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.95277pt\hbox{$Q$}\hskip-3.95277pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raiseb 455.45732pt}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle Q(\bar{\bar{{\bm{r}}}}-\varsigma{\bm{\rho}})\rangle\!\rangle\quad\implies\quad\widehat{\langle\!\langle\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-3.95277pt\hbox{$Q$}\hskip-3.95277pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raiseb 455.45732pt}\rangle\!\rangle}=\frac{2J_{1}}{k_{\perp}\rho}\widehat{Q}.

(K.20)

It follows that
Eq.

K.16
, in terms of Fourier components, can be expressed as

\displaystyle\widehat{\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\m 455.45732pt}}{}^{\mathrm{f}}_{1,s}={}

\displaystyle\frac{q_{s}}{m_{s}}\int\biggl[\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}\left(J_{0}\widehat{(B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}\dot{\bar{\bar{{\bm{R}}}}})_{1}}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{q_{s}}\frac{2J_{1}}{k_{\perp}\rho}\widehat{B_{1,s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}}\right)

\displaystyle+\widehat{\delta\bar{\bar{f}}_{s}}\left(J_{0}{\bm{B}}_{0}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{q_{s}}\frac{2J_{1}}{k_{\perp}\rho}{\bm{B}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}}\right)\biggr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}

(K.21)

for which find that
Eq.

K.3
can be written in terms of Fourier components as follows

\widehat{(B_{s,\shortparallel}^{\star}\dot{\bar{\bar{{\bm{R}}}}})_{1}}=\mathsfbi{R}_{s}\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1},\quad\mathsfbi{R}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=J_{0}\left(-\frac{\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}{\omega}\mathsfbi{K}+\frac{1}{B_{0}}\mathsfbi{B}_{0}\right)+\mathrm{i}\frac{2\bar{\bar{\mu}}J_{1}}{\omega q_{s}k_{\perp}\rho}({\bm{k}}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})({\bm{k}}\times{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}},

(K.22)

where the matrix
\mathsfbi{K}
is such that
Eq.

5.46
holds.
Moreover, the Fourier transform of
B_{1,s,\shortparallel}^{\star}
is given by

\widehat{B_{1,s,\shortparallel}^{\star}}=J_{0}\widehat{B}_{1,\shortparallel}=\frac{J_{0}}{\omega}({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}})\bcdot\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}

(K.23)

by making use of
Eq.

K.13
.

When substituting
Eqs.

K.19
,
K.23
and
K.22
into
Eq.

K.21
we find

\widehat{\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\m 455.45732pt}}{}^{\mathrm{f}}_{1,s}=\mathsfbi{J}_{s}\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1},

(K.24)

where

\displaystyle\mathsfbi{J}_{s}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}={}

\displaystyle\frac{q_{s}}{m_{s}}\int\biggl[\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}\left(J_{0}\mathsfbi{R}_{s}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{q_{s}}\frac{2J_{1}}{k_{\perp}\rho}\frac{J_{0}}{\omega}({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}})({\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}})^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(J_{0}{\bm{B}}_{0}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}+\mathrm{i}\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{q_{s}}\frac{2J_{1}}{k_{\perp}\rho}{\bm{B}}_{0}\times{\bm{k}}\right){\bm{f}}_{s}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\biggr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}.

(K.25)

K.3
The Fourier component of the polarization and magnetization

Computing the Fourier component of the polarization (
4.34
), while substituting Faraday’s law (
K.13
), results in

\widehat{{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}}_{1,s}=\mathsfbi{P}_{s}\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1},\quad\mathsfbi{P}_{s}=\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{B_{0}}\left[\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}+\frac{\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}{\omega}({\bm{k}}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}-\mathsfbi{I}_{3}k_{\shortparallel})\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}},

(K.26)

where we have defined the perpendicular projection matrix
\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}

\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}=\mathsfbi{I}_{3}-{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}\quad\implies\quad\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}{\bm{S}}={\bm{S}}_{\perp}.

(K.27)

For the magnetization (
4.39
) we find

\widehat{{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}_{1,s}=\mathsfbi{M}_{s}\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1},\quad\mathsfbi{M}_{s}=-\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{m_{s}}\left[\frac{m_{s}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}{B_{0}}\left(-\frac{1}{B_{0}}\mathsfbi{B}_{0}\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}+\frac{\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}}{\omega}\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}\mathsfbi{K}\right)-\frac{\bar{\bar{\mu}}}{\omega}\mathsfbi{I}_{\perp}\mathsfbi{K}\right]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}.

(K.28)

K.4
The gyrokinetic susceptibility tensor

We now consider the Fourier component of the linearization of
Eq.

5.40

-\omega^{2}\left(\epsilon_{0}\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}+\sum_{s}\widehat{{\bm{\mathcal{P}}}}_{1,s}\right)=\frac{1}{\mu_{0}}{\bm{k}}\times({\bm{k}}\times\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1})-\omega\sum_{s}{\bm{k}}\times\widehat{{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}_{1,s}+\mathrm{i}\omega\sum_{s}\widehat{\hbox{\hskip 455.45732pt\hskip-2.56944pt\hbox{${\bm{\mathcal{J}}}$}\hskip-2.56944pt\hskip-455.45732pt\raisebox{17.83344pt}{\hbox{$\scalebox{-1}{\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}}$}}\hskip-455.45732pt\hskip 455.45732pt}}_{1,s}

(K.29)

and therefore, when comparing to
Eq.

5.42
, we find that the gyrokinetic susceptibility tensor
\bar{\bar{\mathsfbi{X}}}
is given by

\epsilon_{0}\bar{\bar{\mathsfbi{X}}}=\sum_{s}\mathsfbi{P}_{s}+\frac{1}{\omega}\mathsfbi{K}\sum_{s}\mathsfbi{M}_{s}+\mathrm{i}\frac{1}{\omega}\sum_{s}\mathsfbi{J}_{s},

(K.30)

where the polarization, magnetization and current matrices are given by Eqs. (
K.26
), (
K.28
) and (
K.25
), respectively.

To facilitate the comparison with the results from
(
Hasegawa 1975
;
Zonta et al. 2021
)
we choose the following parallel and perpendicular directions

{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}={\bm{\hat{e}}}_{z},\quad{\bm{k}}=k_{\perp}{\bm{\hat{e}}}_{x}+k_{\shortparallel}{\bm{\hat{e}}}_{z}.

(K.31)

When substituting this into
Eqs.

K.25
,
K.26
,
K.28
and
K.30
we find

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\mathsfbi{X}}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}\begin{pmatrix}\omega-2k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0&k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}\\
0&\omega-2k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0\\
k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}

(K.32)

\displaystyle-\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega^{2}\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}\left(\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}^{2}-\frac{\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}}{2}\right)\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}\begin{pmatrix}-k_{\shortparallel}^{2}&0&k_{\perp}k_{\shortparallel}\\
0&-k_{\shortparallel}^{2}&0\\
k_{\shortparallel}k_{\perp}&0&-k_{\perp}^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{i}\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega^{2}\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}}\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}J_{0}^{2}\begin{pmatrix}0&\omega-k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0\\
k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}-\omega&\mathrm{i}\frac{2k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}J_{1}}{J_{0}}&-k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}\\
0&k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0\end{pmatrix}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega^{2}}\int\frac{k_{\shortparallel}^{2}\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}(k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}-\omega)^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}J_{1}^{2}&\mathrm{i}\frac{\omega}{k_{\shortparallel}}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}J_{0}J_{1}\\
0&-\mathrm{i}\frac{\omega}{k_{\shortparallel}}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}J_{0}J_{1}&\frac{\omega^{2}}{k_{\shortparallel}^{2}}J_{0}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}},

where
\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}=B_{0}/m_{s}
, and the plasma frequency is given by

\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}=q_{s}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}{\epsilon_{0}m_{s}}}.

(K.33)

In deriving
Eq.

K.32
we have made use of partial integration w.r.t.
\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}
and assume that
\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}
vanishes at
\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}=\pm\infty
.

K.5
Drift kinetic susceptibility tensor

In order to compare with the results from
(
Zonta et al. 2021
)
, we now ignore FLR effects by assuming
k_{\perp}\rho\ll 1
such that we may approximate the Bessel functions as follows

J_{0}\approx 1,\quad J_{1}\approx\frac{k_{\perp}\rho}{2}.

(K.34)

This results in the following ZLR limit of
Eq.

K.32

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{\mathsfbi{X}}}^{\mathrm{ZLR}}={}

\displaystyle\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}\begin{pmatrix}\omega-2k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0&k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}\\
0&\omega-2k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0\\
k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0&0\\
\end{pmatrix}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}

(K.35)

\displaystyle-\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega^{2}\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}\left(\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}^{2}-\frac{\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}}{2}\right)\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}\begin{pmatrix}-k_{\shortparallel}^{2}&0&k_{\shortparallel}k_{\perp}\\
0&-k_{\shortparallel}^{2}&0\\
k_{\shortparallel}k_{\perp}&0&-k_{\perp}^{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle+\mathrm{i}\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega^{2}\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}\int\frac{\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}}\begin{pmatrix}0&\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}(\omega-k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel})&0\\
-\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}(\omega-k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel})&\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}^{2}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}&-\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}\\
0&\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}k_{\perp}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}&0\end{pmatrix}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega^{2}}\int\frac{k_{\shortparallel}^{2}\bar{\bar{f}}^{0}_{s}}{\bar{\bar{\mathcal{N}}}_{00,s}(k_{\shortparallel}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\shortparallel}-\omega)^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{4}k_{\perp}^{2}\frac{1}{4\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}&\mathrm{i}\frac{k_{\perp}}{k_{\shortparallel}}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}\frac{\omega}{2\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}}\\
0&-\mathrm{i}\bar{\bar{u}}_{\tau}^{2}\frac{k_{\perp}}{k_{\shortparallel}}\frac{\omega}{2\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}}&\frac{\omega^{2}}{k_{\shortparallel}^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\mathfrak{J}_{00,s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}\bar{\bar{u}}.

It can be verified that this agrees with
(
Hasegawa 1975
, Eq. (2.159))
.

K.6
Gyrokinetic Darwin susceptibility tensor

This is very similar to the gauge-invariant gyrokinetic model, except that we must express the additional term in the Darwin polarization (
5.30
) in terms of the electric field
\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}
.
That is, we must express the perpendicular part of the vector potential in terms of the electromagnetic fields.

From the definition of the electric field (
3.26
) we find that

\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}=-\mathrm{i}{\bm{k}}\widehat{\phi}_{1}+\mathrm{i}\omega\widehat{{\bm{A}}}_{1}\quad\implies\quad{\bm{k}}_{\perp}\bcdot\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}=-\mathrm{i}k_{\perp}^{2}\widehat{\phi}_{1},

(K.36)

after having substituted the Fourier ansatz as well as the constraint (
5.35b
),
{\bm{k}}_{\perp}\bcdot\widehat{{\bm{A}}}_{1}=0
.
It follows that the perpendicular part of the vector potential is given by

\widehat{{\bm{A}}}_{1,\perp}=\frac{\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1,\perp}k_{\perp}^{2}-{\bm{k}}_{\perp}{\bm{k}}_{\perp}\bcdot\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1}}{\mathrm{i}\omega k_{\perp}^{2}}.

(K.37)

Using the specific parallel and perpendicular directions given by
Eq.

K.31
we find

\widehat{{\bm{A}}}_{1,\perp}=\mathsfbi{A}_{\perp}\widehat{{\bm{E}}}_{1},\quad\mathsfbi{A}_{\perp}=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0\\
0&-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{\omega}&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix},

(K.38)

such that when considering
Eqs.

K.30
and
5.30
we find that the gyrokinetic Darwin susceptibility tensor is given by

\bar{\bar{\mathsfbi{X}}}^{\mathrm{Dar}}=\bar{\bar{\mathsfbi{X}}}-\mathrm{i}\omega\sum_{s}\frac{\omega_{\mathrm{p},s}^{2}}{\omega_{\mathrm{c},s}^{2}}\mathsfbi{A}_{\perp},

(K.39)

where we have moreover substituted the definition of the plasma frequency (
K.33
).

We do not discuss the removal of the
{\bm{\hat{b}}}_{0}\bcdot({\bm{E}}_{1}\times{\bm{B}}_{1})
term from
\bar{\bar{H}}_{2,s}
as this does not affect the susceptibility tensor in case of a centred Maxwellian background distribution.
